# Session Log — 2026-11

Append-only. Newest entry at the bottom. One entry per session, written by
the chronicler BEFORE the session's final commit so the entry rides in it.
Format: `## <date> — <issue/trigger> — <outcome>`, then what was
attempted/shipped (commit hash), ratchet movement, decisions, surprises,
friction, next.

- #3243 landed as squash `d5da8182` (PR #3270) and is closed. `lanestatus` at `d5da8182` is unchanged (`datatypes` 1163 / 10, `instance` 26290 / 71, `json` 43 / 0, `schema` 15302 / 96).

- Follow-ups:
  - **The warden's inferred claim that no ungated repeated model group admits two identical-path partitions (`cvc-accept` 3.1) → no counterexample, no issue.** One candidate was probed with `go run ./cmd/goxsd8 gen` at `d5da8182`: `<sequence maxOccurs="unbounded"><b/><a minOccurs="0" maxOccurs="2"/></sequence>` exits 0 and emits `R{Sequence []RSequence}`, a slice of occurrences, so it is ungated, and its required `b` fixes every partition. The claim is still inferred. Why no counterexample turned up, untested: a boundary shift with an identical path needs one particle on both sides of the boundary. That particle has to repeat at both edges, which is the repeating-edge gate, or two particles of the same name have to compete, which `cos-nonambig` forbids.
  - **The `all(a?, any)` case → already in "# Coverage"** (`58bbc9e9`); nothing owed.

## 2026-11-01 — develop (#3287) — accepted: `Generate` shares a document only under one nearest go.mod; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3287`, claimed (`ed50c4ce`) from `origin/main` `e1e9ff64`: mason `5ff8ac0b` (`importPath` returns the nearest go.mod's directory beside the import path, carried on `unit.mod`; `agreeingOwner` skips an owner under another go.mod, so the earliest same-module owner that agrees is chosen; `Generate`'s sharing paragraph, gen `-help`/`doc.go` and `codegen/doc.go` "# Determinism" state the condition; `TestGenerateSharesWithinModule`, five rows); then `a53f45c4`, the warden's two comment-only findings, applied by the orchestrator. Arbiter **accept**: gate parts 1–4 exit 0 (part 4 525s); Ratchet: unchanged on all lanes, no bank commit. This entry lands in a LOG-only commit on the branch.
- Cost — 2 warden (1 revise), 1 mason, 2 arbiter, 1 chronicler. No oracle: `## Spec` is n/a. The warden rounds are the pre-flight and the diff review; the arbiter rounds are the grounding and the verdict. No repair round, no rejects, no lost rounds.
- Session: no RESUME; nothing lost. Thread: GROUNDING (arbiter) issuecomment-6561855564; warden pre-flight RULING issuecomment-6561874235; MASON issuecomment-6562043559; warden DIFF REVIEW (revise) issuecomment-6562083529; VERDICT (accept) issuecomment-6562307494.
- Behaviour change, for callers: a layout whose cross-module pair built through a `replace` directive, a published `require` or a `go.work` using both modules used to get `type A = x.A`, an alias of the earlier module's type; it now gets a type of its own (`type A string`, likewise every shared declaration), so the two packages' types are no longer identical. No knob restores the alias (RULING 4: Generate cannot see how the importer's build resolves the owner's module, and an opt-in would be an export with no consumer, STYLE T5).
- Decisions:
  - **Define, not refuse** (RULING 1). A `CheckTargets` refusal reads no Schema, so it would refuse every pair under different nearest go.mods, sharing or not; define breaks no layout that builds today except the alias layouts above. `CheckTargets`, its doc and gen's exit codes are unchanged.
  - **The condition compares go.mod directories, not module paths** (RULING 2): absolute, cleaned, no symlink resolution, so a mis-spelt directory only withdraws sharing.
  - **A candidate filter, not a veto** (RULING 3). The three-target row (o1 under the outer go.mod, o2 and t under `./sub`) tells the two apart: the mason's veto mutation fails only that row; removing the filter fails all five.
  - **The diff review's findings were comment-only**, so the orchestrator applied them as `a53f45c4` instead of opening a mason repair round: the fallback clause restating the module condition a fourth time was deleted, and "# Determinism" now says "which of them have the target's nearest go.mod".
  - **Adjacent #3288 and #3289 stay open.** The mason absorbed #3289's "agree" clause only; #3288's split and verbless-sentence fix, and #3289's coupling sentence, one-run-per-pair sentence and paragraph move were not absorbed.
- Surprises: the grounding found the defect runs both ways (owner in the nested module, importer outside it), and that the Acceptance's `x.Common` names the owner's package only when its `-out` is `./o/x`.
- Friction: none the documents did not predict. The permission classifier refused the mason's end-to-end `goxsd8 gen` + `go build` script, and the arbiter ran it at the verdict, where it passed (ROUTINES's refused-command bullet, #1791). Reports came back as hand-backs.
- Next — discharged before landing: grounding, pre-flight, implementation, diff review and its corrections, verdict and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass: #3288 and #3289 against the rewritten paragraphs; the behaviour change above, for whoever writes release notes.

Nine `.go` files under `cmd/goxsd8`. The new `subcommandUsageError(stderr, name, msg)` prints the diagnosis, `usage: ` plus that subcommand's synopsis line, then `helpPointer`. All 29 exit-2 sites inside a subcommand use it: I/O faults, gen's per-pair `-out` refusals and `reportGenerate`'s non-decline lines included. `usageError` keeps the two-line form for `run` → `diagnose` only, and `dispatchCases` is byte-unchanged. `synopsis(name)` reads the line from `usage`, anchored on its two-space indent, and panics unless exactly one line matches. `TestUsageCoversContract` asserts one line per name in `subcommands`. The usage table's code-2 row gains one sentence; doc.go was regenerated. `go tool surface` unchanged. Arbiter **accept** at `d3d056f6`: gate parts 1–4 exit 0 (part 4 902.947s); Ratchet: unchanged, `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run exit 0, nothing banked. This entry lands in a LOG-only commit on the branch.

- Decisions: **grounding ruled what "usage diagnosis" covers**, which the body left open. Every pair `usageError` prints after dispatch gets the synopsis, I/O faults included, because the tree has no second classification to narrow by; gen's exit-4 declines get none. It also found the hazard that shaped the lookup: `usage`'s column-0 prose lines `goxsd8 parse compiles …` and `goxsd8 validate assesses …` share the synopsis prefix once trimmed, so a trim-then-prefix lookup is unanchored. It required literal test expectations, since a pin computed by the production lookup cannot fail. The mason chose two named functions over one `usageError` with an empty-name sentinel, so the pre-subcommand path cannot reach the lookup. Four mutations were executed (synopsis dropped, lookup unanchored, synopsis line deleted, name swapped); the arbiter re-ran two. The arbiter ruled the panic acceptable in `package main`: only an edit to `usage` the gate already rejects can reach it.
- Surprises: none.
- Friction: **a built binary committed by accident.** The mason's first commit `ac368427` carried `cmd/goxsd8/goxsd8`, the output of a bare `go build` in the package directory; `.gitignore` ignores `bin/` but not that path. Option (a): a use gets the value type when `{required}` is true, or when its effective value constraint (§3.5.4 key-evc) is present and its declaration is not a built-in xsi: one (key-dflt-att clauses 2–4, tested by namespace, exact per no-xsi §3.2.6.4). Also absorbed: `xsd.Schema.EffectiveValueConstraint`'s doc, whose "Two callers" was already false at base. `go tool surface -base origin/main`: unchanged. Arbiter **accept** on round 1 at `aa282513`, no findings: gate parts 1–4 exit 0 (part 4 PASS 916s); Ratchet: unchanged, ratchet run, tree clean, nothing to bank. `gencensus` generated 11866 (declined 2), unchanged; `decodecensus` rejected codegen's own struct 0, decoded 143, decoded where validate rejects 0. This entry lands in a LOG-only commit on the branch.

Mason ~28 min, arbiter verdict ~32 min. No repair, no reject. Prediction (no lane imports `codegen` or `codec`, so every lane unchanged) held.

- Decisions:
  - **The warden ruled (a) over (b)** (keep `*T` for a future encoder): under `*T` nil is a state `Decode` never produces, so a hand-built nil and `&<constraint value>` encode one infoset fact twice (STYLE T1, D3); the pointer does not distinguish absent from present-with-the-value either; and an encoder can omit by comparing against the schema's constraint, which it reads as codec does. No encoder is filed.
  - **The xsi: arm keeps `*T`**, since key-dflt-att clause 4 supplies nothing there. Mason probed `<xs:attribute ref="xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation" default="a.xsd"/>`: it compiles, and `valueconstraint.golden` pins `NoNamespaceSchemaLocation *string`. The verdict found no-xsi's Note calls such a use legal and of no effect, which settles reachability.
  - **Grounding found three body claims stale or under-counted, and the orchestrator restated the body**: the decode rows that move are eight, not five (codec's item 6 compares field Go types, so every `opt{A string; B *string}` row moves); `codec/decode.go`'s cited lines had drifted (401-403 → 422-425); and `valueconstraint.xsd` already carried the optional default rows, which flip rather than being added — only the optional fixed row is new.
  - **Mutations pinned every row**, re-run by the arbiter: narrowing removed fails `Lang`, `Note`, `Currency`; xsi: exclusion removed fails `NoNamespaceSchemaLocation`; codec reverted fails nine `TestDecodeAttributeValueConstraint` rows (the eight moved plus the new `ErrTypeMismatch` row).
- Surprises: with only codec reverted, `decodecensus` reports rejected codegen's own struct 3 while gate part 1 stays green, since no codec test imports codegen. The census is the only instrument that sees the two packages disagree, as the ruling predicted.
- Friction: first sighting — the session's initial `git fetch --prune origin` (develop.md step 1) ran past the Bash tool's 120s default and finished as a background command; nothing documents its duration or says to run it in the background.

  - **Charged gets a helper, not a type** (R1): `ViolationsOf` follows `xsderr.RuleOf`/`LocOf`, and the warden rejected both a documented `Unwrap() []error` route (it fails once a caller wraps with `%w`) and an exported `*ChargedError` (two identifiers where one suffices, T5). `chargedError.Unwrap() []error` stays, unnamed as a route, so item 3's "errors.As … reach the first violation alone" holds.

  - **Two Decode errors joined by a caller**: `ViolationsOf` returns the first `chargedError` `errors.As` finds. The warden's diff review ruled that right (RuleOf/LocOf's first-in-chain convention; Decode never returns two) and it stays out of the doc until a test row exercises it.

The diff is 8 files, +266/-67; `go tool surface -base origin/main` prints `surface: unchanged`. Arbiter **accept** at round 1 on `bc63cf14` (code at `45f01c28`): RATCHET: unchanged; RATCHET-STATE: ratchet run, tree clean, nothing to bank; FINDINGS: none. Gate green; part 4 took 494.545 s. This entry lands in a LOG-only commit on the branch.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 1 cartographer, 2 warden, 1 mason, 2 arbiter. The oracle and the grounding arbiter ran in the earlier session. This session ran the cartographer's body re-statement, the warden pre-flight and diff review, the mason round (no repair) and the round-1 accept (no reject).
- Sessions: two. The first session grounded the issue and ended at the grounding checkpoint (RESUME issuecomment-6628187322). This session found the lease EXPIRED and took it over (TAKEOVER issuecomment-6630187366). Thread on #3335: GROUNDING issuecomment-6628184852; WARDEN pre-flight issuecomment-6630368750; MASON issuecomment-6630791435; WARDEN diff review issuecomment-6631053176; ARBITER issuecomment-6631463238. The re-sighting it came from is issuecomment-6606307244.
- Decisions:
  - **The layer is `value`, not `validate`'s `cvc-type` wrapper** (grounding). The inheriting type is not always the governing type (list item, union member, simple-content `{content type}`), and `validate` never sees which facet failed. `compile` (`value/facets.go`:550) holds both `st` and each `Declaring()`.
  - **Arm A**: the body widened to `enumFacet.CheckValue` and `boundFacet.CheckValue`. Before this, no value-facet rejection named any type, so a test row had no relation to assert.
  - **Own vs inherited is decided by facet kind and position, never by QName equality** (pre-flight item 1, bar (c), #552). A facet is own iff `st.OwnFacets()` holds that kind and no later entry of `eff` has it. This rests on `checkSTProps` (st-props-correct clause 4) and on `overlayFacet` appending the own entry last. The warden's 18641-entry randomized differential found 0 disagreements. Mutation M2 (QName equality), run by both the mason and the arbiter, fails exactly the anonymous-heir row.
  - **Own enumeration and bound rejections now name the type too**, as `of <D>` (pre-flight item 3). Otherwise a bound rejection under a list item or union member still names no type. The body's Surface was widened to say so.
  - Absorbed beyond the body: a new `value/enummessage_test.go` (no row pinned the enumeration rejection before), and the Narrower-vs-Code later-entry row. The arbiter ruled both sound.
- Surprises: the faults at `:900`/`:1047`, which the pre-flight only inferred were reachable, are reachable. Mason confirmed it with the `enumHeir` and `boundHeir` rows.
- Friction:
  - **The first session ended at the grounding checkpoint before the body was re-stated, with Acceptance bullet 3 false as written.** That left the cartographer re-statement, and the choice between arm A and arm B, to whoever took over. The RESUME named this as step 1, so this session spent no extra round. It did pay a lease expiry and a takeover before the work could go on. First sighting. Search words: ended at grounding checkpoint, body unrestated, false acceptance bullet carried across sessions.
  - **The warden's pre-flight item 7 ruled one commit, and the mason split the work into three** because its brief carried WORKFLOW's commit-as-you-go clause (#1443). The result is `79b38156`, a commit that is red on purpose. The warden diff review and the arbiter each recorded the split as an observation, not a finding: the squash lands one green commit. No round was lost. The two directions conflict: a pre-flight ordering ruling against WORKFLOW's commit clause, which owns the subject. First sighting. Search words: pre-flight commit ordering, one-commit ruling, commit-as-you-go, deliberate red commit.

Gate green; part 4 `ok … conformance 511.317s`, no improved, regressed or vanished lines. `go tool decodecensus` on `9dfe14c6` (the arbiter's measurement): decoded **1484 → 1504**; declined **13115 → 13095**; the element-ref row **742 → 74**, renamed from `an <element ref> particle` to `an <element ref> particle to a head with {abstract} true or a substitution group of another non-abstract declaration`; `rejected codegen's own struct` **0**; `decoded where validate rejects` **0**. The warden's two are the pre-flight (revise, a shape ruling for the mason) and the diff review (revise, one doc sentence, no code change); the orchestrator transcribed that sentence as `9dfe14c6`, so no repair round ran. Mason round 1 ran about 27 minutes. Thread on #3374: GROUNDING issuecomment-6641189749; WARDEN pre-flight issuecomment-6641274194; MASON issuecomment-6641838849; WARDEN diff review issuecomment-6642072750; ARBITER issuecomment-6642514923.
- Decisions:
  - **A ref field gets its own value axis.** `field` carries a closed `valueType` (`valueString` for local elements and attribute uses, `valueNamed` for an `<element ref>`), derived from `ScopeVariety() == xsd.ScopeGlobal`, codegen's own gate. `field.accepts` matches a ref field as any named type whose underlying type is string, never by name; `string` itself does not pass at a ref site, so the two sets stay disjoint, which `bindChoice`'s shared-branch test and `WithBranches` matching depend on (pre-flight; arbiter ruled item 6 one consistent rule). `field.store` builds from `dst.Type()` and does not branch on the axis.
  - **The predicate is codegen's `substitutionSum`**: `substitutable` deletes the abstract members of `Schema.SubstitutionGroup` and declines unless the head is left alone. `Schema.SubstitutionGroup` decides cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec clauses 1–2.3, so Decode carries no separate 2.1 test; rows pin 2.1 and 2.3, closing the oracle's open point and the arbiter's unmeasured clause-2.3 case.
  - **Constructs renamed, not minted, at their iota positions**: `constructElementRef` → `constructSubstitutionRef`; `constructComplexElement` is now named "a particle's element declaration of complex type", true of a referenced top-level declaration as well.
  - **Deviation accepted by warden and arbiter**: the substitution test is the first check of `localElement`, renamed `elementField`, rather than living in `particleElement`, so the decline subject is `declSubject(head)`. Mutation M3 pins the order.
  - Mutation M4 (valueType dropped from the shared-branch test) survived the first fixture; `32a0f577` reshaped it until it failed.
- Surprises: the lift's figures matched the arbiter's grounding-time throwaway probe exactly (74, +20). It exposed the lifted heads' later first declines: list 4940→4949, other primitive 4794→4820, union 264→273, nillable 28→40, type table 22→32, and the complex-type row 852→1434.
- Friction:
  - **Notes hypothesis falsified at grounding.** The body said the ref's field "decodes unchanged"; the arbiter's probe showed codegen emits the head's named type over string (`A`, `*A`, `[]A`), which `field.matches` and `field.store` rejected, so the lift owed a field-match change the body had not scoped. First sighting.
  - **Acceptance restated at grounding**, for the second develop entry running (#3373's entry): the census bullet's row label could not survive the rename its guard bullet asked for, the complex-type construct's name was false for a referenced head, two `TestDecodeDeclines` rows needed re-targeting, and the docs bullet named too few sites. Second sighting; the post-land pass files it.
- Next — discharged before landing: grounding and the body restatement, the pre-flight, implementation, the diff review and its sentence, the verdict and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass:

No bank commit.
- **M10's measure, the two figures the Acceptance asks this entry to quote**, from the arbiter's `go tool decodecensus` on `4032c1a8`: the `a list simple type` row **4949 → gone**; decoded **4403 → 7783** (+3380, the grounding's upper bound reached). Declined 10196 → 6816; `an atomic simple type whose primitive is none of …` 1886 → 3446; `a union simple type` 273 → 282; rejected codegen's own struct **0**; decoded where validate rejects **0**.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 2 warden, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter. The arbiter's two are the grounding and the accept; no reject. The warden's two are the pre-flight (revise, three design defects ruled R1–R7) and the diff review (revise, 2 findings); the repair answered the diff review, not an arbiter verdict. A first warden send returned `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` and did no work; it is not a round. The repair bought types that make two illegal states unrepresentable: `atomicDecline` can no longer be handed a list variety, and no result carries both a decline and a plain error. Thread on #3404: GROUNDING issuecomment-6663448401; WARDEN pre-flight issuecomment-6663593737; MASON issuecomment-6664234832; WARDEN diff review issuecomment-6664363889; MASON repair issuecomment-6664573033; ARBITER accept issuecomment-6664972425.
- Decisions:
  - **The list layer sits on `scalar`, once** (pre-flight R1): an optional `item *listItem` set only by `newScalar`, with no list flag. For a list, `atom` is the item's atomic, and `scalar.goType()` gives A or `[]A`. `accepts`, `describe` and `simpleRoot.check` read it; the per-token conversion keeps `atom.bare()`.
  - **The shared-branch test was redesigned, not extended** (R1b). The old (name, shape, value, atom) tuple is wrong both ways once lists decode. The warden's P3 probe showed a false `constructSharedBranch` for xs:int beside a list of xs:int, and a missed one for repeated xs:int beside a list of xs:int (both `{X []int32}`), which would have rejected codegen's own struct. `sharesBranch` compares the composed Go type for `valueBare`, and shape and Go type separately for `valueNamed`.
  - **`constructList` is retired, not narrowed** (the arbiter's grounding, R2). `checkListGraph` (cos-st-restricts 2.1) leaves an item only an atomic type, `constructUnion` or `constructOtherPrimitive`, so a list declines by its item's construct. A list item that is itself a list is a plain error, unreachable after Finalize. The grounding ruled false the Acceptance guard that had rows :49 and :101 "move with the narrowed construct": at `e3ac1bbf` they are :50 and :102, both have an xs:string item, and both now decode.
  - **Split on U+0020 only, after whiteSpace runs once under the list type** (R4). `strings.Fields` would split a U+00A0 inside an xs:string item, and a bare `strings.Split` gives one empty token for the empty list. The mapping is looked up only when there is at least one token, so the empty list never reaches item 8. A failing token stays a `*stageError` that names its 1-based position and the item type "of" the list.
  - **A decline by the item's construct names the list's {item type definition} and its Loc** (R5), so a list of xs:hexBinary does not read as an atomic type in the message.
  - **Arbiter rulings:** the decode rows live in `codec/list_test.go`, not the Acceptance's `codec/decode_test.go`, and the file name is not a bar. The item 5 reflow that overlaps #3431 is not a finding, because the Notes settle which issue rebases. The mason's doc edits to Decode's opening value paragraph, outside the brief's item set, were approved by the warden as two of R7's own `atomic Go type` sites.
- Surprises: the census reached its upper bound exactly. All 3380 lists of a decoded item decode, so items 7 and 8 at fill turned none away. `TestDecodeEmptyLists` measured that a present-empty `*[]A` is a non-nil pointer to a zero-length slice and an absent one is nil, and that a present whitespace-only value with a default gives the empty list, not the default.
- Friction:
  - **A warden ruling row's example text was garbled.** R4's row read `"a b c"` → `["a b", "c"]`, which does not follow from its stated reason. The orchestrator read the intent as a U+00A0 inside a token, and the mason built the row that way. The M4 mutation (`strings.Fields`) confirms it. It cost no round. This is a first sighting. Search words: garbled ruling row, invisible character lost from a verdict's example.
  - The first warden send's `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` was raised because the grounding file lacked its `issuecomment-<id>` heading; it was resent, at no round's cost. This is the brief-part assembly class #3314 tracks. The mason's and the arbiter's interim "waiting on background work" notices earn no line.

The new `a particle's element declaration of type xs:anyType` row 287; empty content 818, simple content 188, mixed content 128. `rejected codegen's own struct` **0**, `decoded where validate rejects` **0**. The six self-reaching corpus cases (ctZ003, assert020/021, over006, d4_3_15v14/15) finish without overflow.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 2 warden, 1 mason, 2 arbiter. The arbiter's two are the grounding and the accept; no reject, no repair round. The warden's two are the pre-flight (revise, five design findings ruled R1–R8 and F1) and the diff review (revise, 1 finding, T5), whose replacement text the orchestrator transcribed in `879eb59d` (develop.md step 5). A first pre-flight send returned `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` and did no work; it is not a round. The grounding's arbiter round took about 27 minutes and the mason's about an hour. Thread on #3434: GROUNDING issuecomment-6666053491; WARDEN pre-flight issuecomment-6666274124; MASON issuecomment-6667352770; WARDEN diff review issuecomment-6667517648; ARBITER accept issuecomment-6668092203.
- Decisions:
  - **Recursion ends by knot-tying at by-name edges, not a visited set** (pre-flight R3, STYLE D4). Each `bindRun` holds two `unitIndex` lookups, `elements` (the root and every top-level declaration reached by `<element ref>`) and `types` (a local declaration's `TypeDefinitionRef`), and stores a unit's entry when it is allocated, before its content is bound. Inline anonymous types bind under no key. No removal on failure: any item-5 decline aborts the whole bind. The grounding's probe had shown a naive recursive bind overflowing on 6 corpus cases.
  - **Item 6's check ties the same knot by `(complexUnit, reflect.Type)`** (R6), not by one Go type per unit, which would break item 6's same-shape promise. The mason's mutations show the check key by unit alone fails only the `struct{R struct{}}` probe; the R6 same-shape row does not discriminate it, and the account says so.
  - **One constructor, `bindRun.complex`, builds the root's and every nested unit** (R2) and decides struct or bare interface up front from `len(ct.AttributeUses())` and `len(group.Particles())`, so the unit can be allocated before its content. The warden's diff review and the arbiter both read it as equal to the base's after-the-fact rule while every attribute use gives a field or declines and every particle gives an alternative or declines.
  - **Item 5's order at depth** (R4): the declaration's type table and nillable, then xs:anyType by name, then {abstract} true, then the nested unit's own constructs. `constructComplexElement` is renamed in its iota slot to `constructAnyTypeElement`; `constructAbstractTypeElement` is added (F1: codegen never emits an abstract type's struct). Every item-5 decline at any depth stays charged to the document element, for CheckDecode parity (R5).
  - **A shared branch name with a nested alternative declines conservatively** (R8), and a nested alternative is selected by its field's name first, so no failed trial leaves a knot entry.
  - **The grounding corrected the Acceptance**: the stale baseline `4990afa7` replaced by `385ec42f`; the empty `<xs:sequence/>` rows (`codec/decline_test.go`:94-95, :99-100) move to empty content and :102-103 to mixed content, not to decode; a nested choice's optional shape is the bare interface, not `*A`; and a recursion row was added.
- Surprises: the census rose by +277, 8 short of the bound. One decline is the conservative shared-name rule (`d3_4_6v06i`, under "two choice alternatives whose branch structs have one shape"); the account does not attribute the other 7. A mutation showed the `choiceRoot` knot untested until the interface-only self-reach row (`9654625b`) was added.
- Friction:
  - The pre-flight's `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` was raised because the grounding part lacked its `issuecomment-<id>`; resent at no round's cost. This is the brief-part assembly class #3314 tracks.
  - Round durations, recorded and not filed: the grounding's arbiter round about 27 minutes (it built and reverted a recursive-bind probe to split the row's 1435), the mason's about an hour on a +1126/−315 diff. Both are the price of the slice's size, not of a document's gap.

## 2026-11-06 — develop (#3437) — accepted after one repair round: codec decodes the date, time and duration datatypes into codegen's datatype structs; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3437`: mason round 1 `59c4f365`, `460c49a4` and `ad609280` (merged at `623a1e29`), repair `05269d3a`. `codec/temporal.go` converts the nine date, time and duration primitives and dateTimeStamp, dayTimeDuration and yearMonthDuration into codegen's datatype structs, through `convertTo`, the one `Convert` site. The decline's String names the fifteen primitives it now reads, at all seven sites. Arbiter **accept** at `05269d3a`: gate parts 1-4 all exit 0, part 4 `--- PASS: TestConformance (423.66s)`; `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` left `conformance/testdata/expectations/` clean. RATCHET: unchanged, nothing to bank, no bank commit. `go tool surface -base origin/main`: unchanged. `go tool decodecensus` at `05269d3a` (the arbiter's four figures): other-primitive row 3512 → **831**; decoded 8068 → **10746**; `rejected codegen's own type` **0**; `decoded where validate rejects` **0**. Declined 6531 → 3853. The rise of 2678 is inside the grounding's bound of 2679; the one case short, `MS-Additional2006-07-15/addD004a`, goes to xsi:type.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 2 warden, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter. The arbiter's two are the grounding and the accept; the warden's two are the pre-flight approve and the diff review's revise. Thread on #3437: GROUNDING issuecomment-6690042670 (oracle and arbiter); warden pre-flight approve issuecomment-6690172180 (S1–S10); MASON issuecomment-6691081816; warden diff review **revise** issuecomment-6691354685 (one finding, D3); MASON repair issuecomment-6691628715; ARBITER accept issuecomment-6692116589.
- Decisions:
  - **The grounding re-stated the body's baselines at claim: 3512 / 8068 / 6531**, because four codec commits had moved them since the body was written. The share this issue holds is **2681** measured, not the path-grep bound of 2548 the PLAN row prints.
  - **D3: `datatypeNames` is the one arm-to-datatype encoding.** Round 1 stored each of the twelve temporal pairings twice, as arm → name and as QName → arm in `chainAtomics`/`primitiveAtomics`. The repair builds `datatypeAtomics` from the array once (an index never iterated, STYLE D2) and `chainAtomic` consults it at each step, so the nearest builtin on the chain still wins, as in `codegen.builtinType`. A 135-input differential against `623a1e29` answered identically.
  - **S5's deviation accepted:** `ConvertibleTo(mirror)` already implies a struct, so the `Kind() == Struct` guard decided nothing; the mirrored-list arm keeps its load-bearing `Kind() == Slice` test.
  - **yearMonthDuration's conversion trace record is kept** (warden and arbiter agree): it is a stage that runs and can fail as `stageConversion`, rule `dt-lexical-mapping` (§2.3, §3.4.26.1's note).
  - `datatypeAtomics` is built by a function literal, not `init()`; STYLE has no rule, and `tools/restatements/match.go` sets the precedent.
- Surprises: **`builtin/strict` rejects the duration forms `.5S` and `1.S`**, which the §3.3.6.2 `unsignedDecimalPtNumeral` production admits and codec's grammar now reads. Codec's acceptance is sound (the arbiter's spec read); whether strict false-rejects is an oracle question.
- Friction:
  - Scheduled iteration; nothing in it the documents did not predict.
  - The mason and the arbiter each ended once with an interim "waiting on its own background work" notice before the real report: docs/ROUTINES.md's predicted case, no round lost.
  - **The issue body's baselines were stale at claim** (3512 / 8068 / 6531 re-stated by the grounding). The grounding absorbed it at no round's cost; first sighting of a body's census baselines outrun by same-epic landings between filing and claim, recorded and not filed.

## 2026-11-06 — develop (#3469) — accepted on the first round: ParseReport's error shapes documented, xsderr's rule-catalog notes moved to gen.go; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3469`: mason `b15ddf2e` and `a8381f57`, merged forward at `7580a858`. `ParseReport`'s doc replaces its old error paragraph with four arms, and `Parse` inherits them: a catalog-rule `*xsderr.Error`, which covers a composed document that resolves but is unreadable or not well-formed, re-charged under the directive's rule; a `RuleXMLWellFormed` `*Error` for an unreadable or not-well-formed root; a plain error for s4s grammar faults, a non-`<schema>` root, a derivation the parser cannot yet map (#1883) and resolver faults; and `*builtin.MissingPrimitivesError` from an incomplete `WithBackend`. `Root`'s and `RootAt`'s docs now point at those arms. `xsderr`'s "# The rule catalog" keeps one sentence, and its maintenance text is merged into `gen.go`'s file comment. "# Using xsderr" gains the s4s sentence (#1371). `TestParseReportErrorShapes` pins the arms that were unpinned or pinned only at the CLI. Arbiter **accept** at `7580a858` against base `a2431a5e`: gate parts 1-4 exit 0, part 4 443s with no improved or unbanked lines; every changed line in a non-test `.go` file is a `//` comment. RATCHET: unchanged, ratchet run, tree clean, nothing to bank. `go tool surface -base origin/main`: unchanged.
- Cost — 1 mason, 2 arbiter. The arbiter's two are the grounding (no oracle block, `## Spec` n/a) and the accept; no warden, since pre-flight was not owed. Thread on #3469: GROUNDING issuecomment-6691993313; MASON issuecomment-6692513856; VERDICT accept issuecomment-6692856495.
- Decisions:
  - **The new paragraph replaces `parse.go`:145-150** (GROUNDING A1a) rather than sitting beside it, which would have left two contradicting statements.
  - **The #1883 derivation decline is named as a plain-error arm.** It is true today and pinned, and it is worded as temporary without a `#N` (T5a). The mason flagged it for scrutiny, and the arbiter ruled it sound.
  - **`RuleComponentInvariant` reaching a caller is stated as a parser defect**, a statement of design. A probe over the suite's 15470 `.xsd` documents as roots produced none.
  - **Absorbed (WORKFLOW scope):** the T5a breaches in `xsderr`'s package doc (a STYLE ID, spec line locators, `#966`, two unexported function names), and `Root`/`RootAt`'s "plain I/O error" prose, which the new arms contradicted.
- Surprises: none beyond the Friction lines.
- Friction:
  - **The issue's Goal claim that `ParseReport` "says nothing" of its error shape was false.** `parse.go`:145-150 already held a paragraph that was wrong twice. The grounding re-stated the body, at no round's cost. This is a first sighting of an issue body that asserts a doc is absent when it is present and wrong; recorded, not filed.
  - **The issue's arm list missed two shapes**: `*builtin.MissingPrimitivesError` (grounding A1e) and the #1883 decline (the mason, from the code). Both were absorbed in the round.
  - **`origin/main` moved mid-session** (#3437 landed at `a2431a5e`), and the branch merged forward cleanly at `7580a858`. This is the process working.
- Next — discharged before landing: grounding, implementation, the accept and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass:
  - Read back #3469 closed by the squash.
  - **File the stale "plain" comments** in `conformance/schema.go`:551-552, :754 and :968-969 and `conformance/instancehints.go`:636-637. They say `ParseSet` answers an unreadable root with a plain error; it is a `RuleXMLWellFormed` `*Error`. The reasoning holds, and only the word is wrong.
  - **File the loose `xsderr` sentence** "IsValidRule tests membership": it does not mention the two sentinels `IsValidRule` accepts.
  - **Fold onto #2953/#1593:** `parser/doc.go`:318-349 still calls the s4s class plain while listing its sites, which is the per-construct catalog that sweep owns.

## 2026-11-06 — develop (#3473) — accepted on the first round: codec decodes empty-content complex types; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3473` at `7da6776e` (base `ed58cbbd`): mason `310934fc`, `2844704f`, `4c3c3688` and `f72bed88`, merged at `cfa29b41`. `contentGroup` now takes `xsd.ElementContent`, and `(*bindRun).complex` switches on the sealed `ContentType()`: empty content binds a zero-field `*sequence` and skips the choice-root test, simple content declines as before, element content goes to `contentGroup`. `constructEmptyContent` and its name row are deleted. `TestDecodeEmptyContent` (7 rows), `TestEmptyTypesAreCodegens` and `TestDecodeEmptyContentRefusesCharacters` are in `codec/empty_test.go`; the `decline_test.go` rows that named empty content are re-homed as decode rows. Arbiter **accept** at `7da6776e`: gate parts 1-4 exit 0, part 4 PASS (352s) with no improved-case lines. RATCHET: unchanged, ratchet run, tree clean, nothing to bank (conformance links neither `codec` nor `codegen`). `go tool surface -base origin/main`: unchanged. `go tool decodecensus` before (`ed58cbbd`) → after: `empty content` 828 → absent (0); decoded 10746 → **11351** (+605); `rejected codegen's own type` 0 → **0**; `decoded where validate rejects` 0 → **0**; the abstract row 3; the xsi:type row 37 → 35.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 2 warden, 1 mason, 2 arbiter. The arbiter's two are the grounding and the accept; the warden's two are the pre-flight (revise, a binding shape ruling) and the diff review (approve). The mason round took about 27 minutes. Two dispatches answered `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` and are not rounds (Friction). Thread on #3473: GROUNDING issuecomment-6694473147 (oracle and arbiter); WARDEN pre-flight issuecomment-6694542594; MASON issuecomment-6695094744; WARDEN diff review issuecomment-6695311004; ARBITER accept issuecomment-6695805116.
- Decisions:
  - **The shape is the warden's T1 ruling** (issuecomment-6694542594): do not return the zero `xsd.ModelGroup` for empty content, since the zero value means "a component no schema document yields" and declines as `constructNestedGroup`. Narrow `contentGroup` to `xsd.ElementContent` and switch on the sealed sum (STYLE T2) instead. Codegen needed no change, because `codegen/emit.go` already reads content with a comma-ok on `ElementContent`.
  - **Absorbed (WORKFLOW scope): the `{abstract}` test moved from `nested` into `complex`.** The first census after the lift showed `rejected codegen's own type: 1` (`MS-ComplexType2006-07-15/ctL022/instance/ctL022.v`): its document element is of an abstract empty-content type, codegen emits the member's struct, and `bindDecl` never tested `{abstract}`. The document element's type now declines by `constructAbstractTypeElement`, renamed to "an element declaration whose complex {type definition} has {abstract} true". A particle's order is unchanged (xs:anyType, `{abstract}`, content). The warden (issuecomment-6695311004) and the arbiter (issuecomment-6695805116) ruled it in scope: no new rule ID, no new surface, and the acceptance bar fails without it.
  - **The ordering pair's replacement uses `ct mixed="true"`,** so both members still decline after the lift, by `constructNillable` and by `constructMixedContent`, and the pair still pins that the first member in group order is reported.
- Surprises: **the lift exposed a latent defect at the document element.** `bindDecl` had never tested `{abstract}`. The defect existed for element-only abstract roots too, but no suite case reached it, because each such case declined earlier. Only the census's `rejected codegen's own type` row caught it.
- Friction:
  - **The warden and the arbiter each answered `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` once.** The warden pre-flight's brief carried a "key points" grounding, and the diff review's brief carried a MASON part labelled "verbatim essentials". Each was cured by resending the part whole, and no round was counted. This is the brief-part assembly class #3314 (`kind/tooling`) owns, which develop.md's brief rule already calls a placeholder. These are the first sightings on warden dispatches.
- Unmeasured: each of the 605 newly decoded cases beyond the census.
- Next — discharged before landing: grounding, pre-flight, implementation, diff review, the accept and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass:
  - Read back #3473 closed by the squash.
  - **Re-stamp the `docs/PLAN.md` M10 table**, which still shows `empty content | 828`. This is owed to the next `/backlog` stamp under the cartographer's Status rule.
  - **Record both `BRIEF INCOMPLETE` sightings on #3314.**
  - Re-read the next M10 declines' baselines against decoded 11351: the wildcard particle (#3476), the xs:anyType particle and the union simple type.

## 2026-11-07 — develop (#3451) — accepted on the first round: doc-only, both scalar.decode type faults stated unreachable at Decode's return; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3451`: mason `575618e0`, then the merge of origin/main `13ba82b4` (a docs/LOG-only range) and heartbeat `860434d7` (tip). It is doc comments only: `scalar.decode` and `newLexicalStages` in `codec/stage.go`, and the "# Debuggability" section of `codec/doc.go`. `codec.Decode`'s exported doc is untouched. Arbiter **accept** at `860434d7` against origin/main `944eb721`: gate parts 1-4 exit 0, `go tool surface -base origin/main` unchanged, ratchet run (`GOXSD_RATCHET=1`) unchanged, nothing to bank. No lane links codec: `go list -test -deps ./conformance | grep goxsd8/codec` is empty.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter. The arbiter rounds are the grounding (with the oracle) and the accept. The warden pre-flight was not owed: no identifier is new and no exported doc changes. No repair, no reject. Thread on #3451: GROUNDING issuecomment-6703239680 (oracle and arbiter); MASON issuecomment-6703353476; ORCHESTRATOR NOTE (process breach) issuecomment-6703359142; ARBITER VERDICT accept issuecomment-6703485438. The orchestrator's body restatement at grounding has no comment ID: it is a body edit.
- Decisions:
  - **Both faults are UNREACHABLE at Decode's return**, from all three value sources (element content, present attribute, supplied default or fixed value). The arbiter probed this with a scratch test that it deleted:
    - The whiteSpace fault: Finalize does not diagnose it. Validate's String Valid runs the same `value.ApplyWhiteSpace` on every value Decode decodes and records the fault Unevaluated, so Decode returns item 4 (`UndecidedError`) first.
    - The governing-mapping read fault: Finalize refuses an unresolved or absent {variety} or {base type definition} hop, and `ApplyWhiteSpace` and `atomicDecline` read the same hops first.
  - **The Notes' hypothesis was false.** It said a supplied value may escape validate's whiteSpace stage. Validate normalizes default and fixed values per instance too.
  - **The old "a whiteSpace fault is a plain error" wording was false.** The error wraps value's `*xsderr.Error` of rule `RuleComponentInvariant`, which both the arbiter's and the mason's direct-call probes showed. It is corrected at its only two sites, `codec/stage.go` and `codec/doc.go`.
  - **Goal, Spec and Acceptance were restated in the body at grounding.** The Goal's "can return after validate accepts" became an account "as a numbered item or a stated unreachability". The Spec line attributed chain completeness to src-resolve, and the oracle located it at st-props-correct clause 2. The unreachable-fault bullet's "after Finalize" is false for whiteSpace, because Finalize does not diagnose it. No row is owed for an unreachable fault.
  - **The mason cites src-resolve clause 1.1 in `newLexicalStages` and does not cite st-props-correct clause 2.** The arbiter ruled the cite right, because the claim is a per-hop refusal at Finalize. Chain completeness would be a different claim.
- Surprises: the issue's premise inverted. The filing expected at least one reachable fault and a new item in Decode's list. Neither fault is reachable, so the landing corrects two false sentences and adds no item.
- Friction:
  - **The first mason brief answered `BRIEF INCOMPLETE`** because the GROUNDING part was given as condensed "key points". It was re-sent whole. Not a round. This is the brief-part assembly class #3314 (open) owns.
  - **The mason's isolated worktree was gone when its round started**, and it committed `575618e0` directly on local `wip/issue-3451` in the session checkout, with no worktree hand-off. The orchestrator disclosed this as a process breach (issuecomment-6703359142), pushed the commit as-is, and checked the diff against the MASON account. No other writer touched the tree. This is the third sighting of #3489 (open), after #3432 and #3442. It is a new form: the earlier two improvised a fresh worktree, and this one wrote into the shared checkout.
- Unchecked: the mason did not probe a multi-valued whiteSpace {value}, a simple-typed document element, or the governing-mapping fault end to end. The arbiter's grounding probes covered all three.

  - **Rows pin triples, not equality**: 3 vs 3.00 by Scale, 0 vs -0 by Negative, 3E2 as (3,-2) (oracle, `f-precDecLexmap`, `vp-sciPrecision`).
- Surprises: none beyond the grounding's three false premises above.

  - `c19da8c9` adds `TestValidateRendersAnAnonymousTypeChargeAsASentence` in `cmd/goxsd8/validate_test.go`, with subtests `attribute` (:206) and `element` (:731), each pinning the whole stdout for an anonymous `<xs:restriction base="xs:int"/>`.

- Decisions:
  - **A name function, not the format change the issue preferred** (mason; arbiter agreed). Rewriting the format to `is not valid against %s` for both arms would change named-type text, which the Acceptance row pinning a NAMED type guards against. `aValid` keeps the named arm byte-stable and gives only the anonymous arm new words.
  - **Wording `is not valid against an anonymous type definition`** over `is not a valid value of …` (arbiter): a full sentence in STYLE E5's order, fact then citation, and no phrase the named arm lacks. No document owns the wording.
  - **The element row uses an anonymous restriction, not the issue's union** (mason): the union's member causes still print the `?: ` placeholder another issue owns, and pinning it would lock the defect in.

## 2026-11-09 — develop (#3515) — accepted on the first verdict: builtin/strict's precisionDecimal canonical forms re-parse Identical; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3515` at `a50fa310`: `3a71d528` (`scientificCanonical` returns `digits + "E" + adjusted` for a single-digit coefficient, dropping the forced `D.0` mantissa and the pad clamp; `zeroCanonical` returns `"0E" + (−aP)` for ·scale· aP ≤ 0) and `a50fa310` (commentwrap reflow of `canonical`'s doc). `3E2`, `1E7`, `-1E7`, `1E-7`, `1E6`, `5E1`, `0`→`0E0`, `-0`→`-0E0`, `0E5`, `-0E5` and `0E1` now re-parse `Identical`; aP ≥ 1 zeros and multi-digit forms are unchanged. Spec: `f-precDecCanmap` step 5, `f-precDecLexmap`, `vp-sciPrecision`, `vp-pd-sign`, `dt-canonical-mapping`. Arbiter **accept** (issuecomment-6738085909): gate parts 1-4 green (part 4 `ok conformance 478.661s`), **RATCHET: unchanged**, `surface: unchanged`, findings none.
- Cost — 1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter, 1 chronicler. No warden: the grounding's `## Surface` ruled no new identifier and no pre-flight owed. The oracle and the arbiter's grounding ran in parallel; the arbiter's two are that grounding and the accept. The mason's one round made two commits. No repair, no reject, no lost round; no resumable branch, so the session claimed #3515 as Working band row 9 (`c972f63b`). Thread on #3515: GROUNDING issuecomment-6737484362; MASON issuecomment-6737743385; ARBITER VERDICT accept issuecomment-6738085909.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Scope widened past the body's rows** (grounding, arbiter): the body's seven rows were insufficient against its Goal "every finite value". The root cause, `pad = len(digits)-1-frac` = −1 for any single-digit coefficient reaching step 5, also broke `1E-7`, `1E6`, `5E1`, `-5E3` and `1E99999999999999999999`, and `zeroCanonical` broke `0E1`. The bar added `1E-7`, `0E1`, `1E6` and `5E1` as fails-on-revert rows, and per-arm reverts that fail only that arm's rows.
  - **`TestPrecisionDecimalCanonicalUnboundedExponent`'s `1E-99999999999999999999` row changed** (arbiter grounding): the body said that test keeps passing, but the row pinned the non-Identical `1.0E-99999999999999999999` (·scale· 10^20 → 10^20+1). Only that row moved; the zero capacity bound stays.
  - **Zero's forms are implementer-supplied** (oracle): `scientificCanonicalMap(0)` is undefined. `0E5` over `0.0E6` is the shortest-form choice, and `0`→`0E0` is a choice the spec does not make. The sign-of-zero site comment cites `vp-pd-sign`, `f-precDecLexmap` step 3 and `dt-canonical-mapping` and calls `f-precDecCanmap`'s silence on ·sign· a candidate erratum.
  - **#2206 not absorbed** (grounding): the oracle rules steps 3-4 signed, which moves the `-1.23` row and contradicts this issue's "expected strings do not change"; it stays its own landing.
  - Rejected by the mason: keeping `D.0` and raising the exponent changes ·scale·; recomputing the pad from aP adds aP-proportional arithmetic.
- Surprises:
  - The mason's differential probe (1900 lexicals against `origin/main`'s copies, since deleted) changed 266 outputs, every one a form the old code produced that was not `Identical`, and changed no output that already round-tripped. `canonical`'s "every finite form re-parses Identical" rests on that probe set and the test rows.
  - The arbiter's mechanism check: the old pad went negative only for a single-digit coefficient, so the new `len(digits)==1` early return covers exactly the defect class and the clamp was dead once it existed.
- Friction: none the documents did not predict. The mason's first part-4 run failed on an uninitialised `testdata/xsdtests`, mason.md's documented `git submodule update --init` step (#659); it cost no round. The refused chained command and `$` (`-skip '^TestConformance\z'`) are docs/ROUTINES.md's respellings.

  - #2206 (signed vs |nV| in steps 3-4, same function) re-reads its locators against `builtin/strict/precisiondecimal.go` as this landing leaves it (#1636); it moves the `-1.23` row.

## 2026-11-09 — develop (#3538) — accepted after one warden revise: codec splices nested sequence particles through `goshape.Splices`; ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-3538` (claim `486b9895` on origin/main `596ed053`): `274c8b34` (`internal/goshape.Splices`, codegen's splice condition as one implementation; codegen's `(*emitter).splices` and `optional` deleted), `ce77a11e` (the codec lift), `06123474` (a spliced wildcard's ordinal pinned), and the repair `dfd726c4` (wildcard attribution's Matcher and places carried as one value), tip `dfd726c4` under heartbeat `bccff560`. Ratchet: **unchanged**; `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` left `conformance/testdata/expectations/` clean, part 4 `ok` in 457.375s with no improved-but-unbanked line, `surface: unchanged` (`internal/` is skipped; goshape's `# Contract` block lists `Splices`), findings none. `go tool decodecensus` at `dfd726c4` (exit 0, 26361 cases): `a nested model group particle` **249 → 183**; `decoded` **13399 → 13445**; `rejected codegen's own type` **0**; `decoded where validate rejects` **0**; `declined` **1200 → 1154**. Of the 66 cases that left the row, 46 decode and 20 stop at a next decline: special simple type +9, attribute wildcard +7, mixed content +4 (mason's account; the repair is behaviour-free and the arbiter's run reproduced the four figures).
- Cost — 1 oracle, 2 warden (1 revise), 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter, 1 chronicler. No reject, no lost round. The arbiter's two are the grounding and the accept; the warden's are the pre-flight and the diff review. Thread on #3538: GROUNDING issuecomment-6745192774; WARDEN PRE-FLIGHT issuecomment-6745301415; MASON issuecomment-6746092997; WARDEN VERDICT revise issuecomment-6746401616; MASON repair issuecomment-6746645656; ARBITER VERDICT accept issuecomment-6747132048.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The grounding widened Acceptance before the build.** It found `attributeChild` attributes a wildcard child by the Matcher position's first index and refuses positions longer than one, so a wildcard inside a spliced sequence errors and one after it lands in the wrong field (both probed); it added the `sequence(a, sequence minOccurs="0" (b))`, b not emptiable, guard (codegen gives `Sequence *RSequence`), since no listed guard failed if codec spliced every 0..1 sequence; and it named the unexported docs the lift falsifies. The oracle ruled the 0..1 emptiability test asks whether the group is emptiable with the particle taken as 1..1 (§3.8.6.5 `cos-seq-range`, §3.9.6.3 `cos-group-emptiable`), and that the extension row is `sequence(base particle, added)` (`dcl.ctd.ctcc.common` 4.2.3.3), so it binds through the same walk only when each member splices.
  - **One splice condition, in `internal/goshape`** (pre-flight). `Splices(schema, group, occurs) (bool, error)` takes the resolved group, not a `TermOrRef`, so goshape does not become a fourth `<group ref>` resolver (#3551 owns that); codegen calls it too, so there is no twin to drift. Its doc points at `codegen/doc.go` "# Particles" and does not restate the condition (#3345).
  - **Decode arm for wildcards, a flat place table.** `wildcardSlot.places []wildPlace{position, index}`, appended in particle order and looked up with `slices.Equal` (STYLE D2); ordinal `len(places)+1` (D3). The warden rejected a sealed sum mirroring the particle tree as one more type for the same answer. Both [T1] constraints held: one Namer and one `children` map across splice levels, each with a guard row (`Any`, `Any2`; `sequence(a, sequence(b, a))` declines `constructSharedName`).
  - **Two mason departures, both accepted at diff review:** codegen's `optional` deleted (its only use was `splices`), and goshape imports `xsderr` (`xsd.NewOccurs` and `xsd.NewParticle` take an `xsderr.Loc`), with `internal/goshape/doc.go` and the ARCHITECTURE graph row moved. The warden rejected the alternative of callers passing a 1..1 `xsd.Occurs`: a parameter with one legal value is an illegal state the signature would admit (T1).
  - **Warden revise, one [T1] item.** `attributeChild(m, w, …)` took two values legal only as a pair; the repair built `wildAttributor{m, places}` in one place, `(*wildcardSlot).attributor`. The arbiter ruled the alias of the slot's `places` sound: every `attributor` call follows the completed bind.
  - **Scope held:** a nested choice, a choice's model-group alternative and an unspliced nested sequence still decline `constructNestedGroup`; codec's content-level gate is not routed through `Splices`.
- Surprises: `TestDecodeSplicedWildcardOrdinal` exists because the "sequence ordinal forced to 1" mutation survived every row until it was added. The old decline row "nested <group ref>" became "nested <group ref> to a choice group", since a ref to a sequence group now splices.
- Friction:
  - **A warden pre-flight carried a false premise without marking it inferred; the diff review caught it and no round was lost.** It said the splice condition "needs only `xsd`" (taken from the grounding's SURFACE line unchecked; `xsd.NewOccurs`/`xsd.NewParticle` take an `xsderr.Loc`) and that codegen's `optional` "stays for its other uses" (it had none), and the diff review said so itself. This falls under warden.md's rule that every pre-flight claim is marked read, probed or inferred, and matches by kind the unmarked surface prediction in the 2026-11-07 develop (#3439) entry, so this is the second sighting of a pre-flight inference stated as fact. Search words: pre-flight false premise, unmarked inference, needs only xsd, import edge.
  - None else: a lost-nothing session. The two interim "waiting on background work" notices are docs/ROUTINES.md's predicted case and earn no line.
- Next — discharged before landing: grounding, pre-flight, implementation, review, repair, verdict and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass:

Measure and file the nested flattened particle (#3577 owns only the content-level one).
  - **#3556 re-reads `groupPath`** against the splice: its "one step long" premise (`codec/decode.go`) is unprobed under a spliced inline sequence; the pre-flight inferred the key unchanged (§3.3.2.3, cos-element-consistent).
  - **#3551** (one exported xsd `<group ref>` resolver): codec's `modelGroupTerm` is still its own copy, and `goshape.Splices` takes the resolved group, so the resolver stays out of goshape.
